      "solution": "**Identify.** The work functions of Caesium (Cs), Potassium (K) and Sodium (Na) are 2.14 eV, 2.30 eV and 2.75 eV respectively.       "solution": "**Identify.** The amount of energy required to form a soap bubble of radius 2 cm from a soap solution is nearly (surface tension of soap solution = 0.03 N m–1)\n\n**Concept.** A soap bubble has two surfaces; work to form it is $W = 8\\pi r^2 T$.\n\n**Work.** $r=2\\ \\mathrm{cm}=0.02\\ \\mathrm{m}$, $T=0.03\\ \\mathrm{N\\,m^{-1}}$.\n$W = 8\\pi (0.02)^2 (0.03) \\approx 3.02e-4\\ \\mathrm{J}$.\n\n**Option scan.**\n✗ (1) 5.06 × 10–4 J\n✓ (2) 3.01 × 10–4 J\n✗ (3) 50.1 × 10 J –4\n✗ (4) 30.16 × 10–4 J\n\n**Hence proved.** The verified option wording is **(2) 3.01 × 10–4 J**.\n\n**Eliminate.**\n✗ (1) 5.06 × 10–4 J — Does not match the governing NCERT fact/calculation above.\n✗ (3) 50.1 × 10 J –4 — Does not match the governing NCERT fact/calculation above.\n✗ (4) 30.16 × 10–4 J — Does not match the governing NCERT fact/calculation above.",

      "solution": "**Identify.** The venturi-meter works on\n\n**Concept.** A venturi-meter measures flow speed using Bernoulli’s principle (pressure drop in the constriction).\n\n**Work.** Parallel/perpendicular axis theorems and Huygens’s principle are unrelated to venturi flow measurement.\n\n**Option scan.**\n✓ (1) Bernoulli’s principle\n✗ (2) The principle of parallel axes\n✗ (3) The principle of perpendicular axes\n✗ (4) Huygens’s principle\n\n**Hence proved.** The verified option wording is **(1) Bernoulli’s principle**.\n\n**Eliminate.**\n✗ (2) The principle of parallel axes — Parallel-axis theorem is for moment of inertia.\n✗ (3) The principle of perpendicular axes — Perpendicular-axis theorem is for planar lamina MI.\n✗ (4) Huygens’s principle — Huygens’s principle is wave optics.",

      "solution": "**Identify.** A full wave rectifier circuit consists of two p-n junction diodes, a centre-tapped transformer, capacitor and a load resistance. Which of these components remove the ac ripple from the rectified output?\n\n**Concept.** After rectification, a shunt capacitor filter smooths the output by charging/discharging — it removes the AC ripple.\n\n**Work.** Diodes rectify; centre-tap transformer supplies opposite phases; load dissipates power but does not filter ripple.\n\n**Option scan.**\n✗ (1) p-n junction diodes\n✓ (2) Capacitor\n✗ (3) Load resistance\n✗ (4) A centre-tapped transformer\n\n**Hence proved.** The verified option wording is **(2) Capacitor**.\n\n**Eliminate.**\n✗ (1) p-n junction diodes — Diodes convert AC→pulsating DC; they do not smooth ripple.\n✗ (3) Load resistance — Load resistance dissipates power; it is not the filter.\n✗ (4) A centre-tapped transformer — Centre-tapped transformer provides the two half-cycles.",
